<h1 id="layouts-in-views" data-text="Layouts in views" tabindex="-1">Layouts in views</h1>


  

  
    
      
    
  

  <div class="android-page-banner android-page-banner-image-bottom-right">
    <div class="android-page-banner-content">
      <div class="android-page-banner-title">
        Try the Compose way
      </div>
      <div class="android-page-banner-description">
        Jetpack Compose is the recommended UI toolkit for Android. Learn how to work with layouts in Compose.
      </div>
      
      <div class="android-page-banner-cta">
        <a href=https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/layouts/basics>
          Learn Compose layout basics &rarr;
        </a>
      </div>
      
    </div>
    
    <div class="android-page-banner-media">
      <picture>
        
          <source
            class="devsite-dark-theme"
            srcset="https://developer.android.com/static/images/android-compose-ui-logo_dt.png"
            media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)"
            alt="">
        
        <img alt class="android-page-banner-image" src="/static/images/android-compose-ui-logo.png">
      </picture>
    </div>
    
  </div>

<p>A layout defines the structure for a user interface in your app, such as in
an
<a href="/guide/components/activities">activity</a>. All elements in the
layout are built using a hierarchy of
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View">View</a></code> and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/ViewGroup">ViewGroup</a></code>
objects. A <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> usually draws something the user can see and
interact with. A <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> is an invisible container that defines
the layout structure for <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> and other <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code>
objects, as shown in figure 1.</p>

<figure>
  <img src="/static/images/viewgroup_2x.png" alt width="548">
  <figcaption><b>Figure 1.</b> Illustration of a view hierarchy, which defines a
  UI layout.</figcaption>
</figure>

<p><code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> objects are often called <i>widgets</i> and can be one of
many subclasses, such as
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/Button">Button</a></code> or
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/TextView">TextView</a></code>. The
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> objects are usually called <i>layouts</i> and can be one
of many types that provide a different layout structure, such as
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/LinearLayout">LinearLayout</a></code>
or
<a href="/reference/androidx/constraintlayout/widget/ConstraintLayout"><code translate="no" dir="ltr">ConstraintLayout</code></a>.</p>

<p>You can declare a layout in two ways:</p>

<ul>
<li><b>Declare UI elements in XML.</b> Android provides a straightforward XML
  vocabulary that corresponds to the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> classes and subclasses,
  such as those for widgets and layouts. You can also use Android Studio's
  <a href="/studio/write/layout-editor">Layout Editor</a> to build your XML
  layout using a drag-and-drop interface.</p></li>
<li><b>Instantiate layout elements at runtime.</b> Your app can create
  <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> and <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> objects and manipulate their
  properties programmatically. </li>
</ul>

<p>Declaring your UI in XML lets you separate the presentation of your app from
the code that controls its behavior. Using XML files also makes it easier to
provide different layouts for different screen sizes and orientations. This is
discussed further in
<a href="/training/multiscreen/screensizes">Support different screen
sizes</a>.</p>

<p>The Android framework gives you the flexibility to use either or both of
these methods to build your app's UI. For example, you can declare your app's
default layouts in XML, and then modify the layout at runtime.</p>

<aside class="tip">
  <b>Tip:</b> To debug your layout at runtime, use the
  <a href="/studio/debug/layout-inspector">Layout Inspector</a> tool.
</aside>

<h2 id="write" data-text="Write the XML" tabindex="-1">Write the XML</h2>

<p>Using Android's XML vocabulary, you can quickly design UI layouts and the
screen elements they contain, in the same way that you create web pages in HTML
with a series of nested elements.</p>

<p>Each layout file must contain exactly one root element, which must be a
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> or <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> object. After you define the root
element, you can add additional layout objects or widgets as child elements to
gradually build a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> hierarchy that defines your layout. For
example, here's an XML layout that uses a vertical <code translate="no" dir="ltr">LinearLayout</code> to
hold a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">TextView</code> and a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Button</code>:</p>

<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="XML">&lt;?xml<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>version="1.0"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>encoding="utf-8"?>
&lt;LinearLayout<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>xmlns:android="http://schemas.android.com/apk/res/android"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:layout_width="match_parent"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:layout_height="match_parent"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:orientation="vertical"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span>&lt;TextView<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>android:id="@+id/text"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:layout_width="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:layout_height="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:text="Hello,<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>I<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>am<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>a<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>TextView"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>/>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span>&lt;Button<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>android:id="@+id/button"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">            </span>android:layout_width="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">            </span>android:layout_height="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">            </span>android:text="Hello,<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>I<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>am<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>a<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>Button"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>/>
&lt;/LinearLayout></pre></devsite-code>

<p>After you declare your layout in XML, save the file with the
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">.xml</code> extension in your Android project's <code translate="no" dir="ltr">res/layout/</code>
directory so it properly compiles.</p>

<p>For more information about the syntax for a layout XML file, see
<a href="/guide/topics/resources/layout-resource">Layout resource</a>.</p>

<h2 id="load" data-text="Load the XML resource" tabindex="-1">Load the XML resource</h2>

<p>When you compile your app, each XML layout file is compiled into a
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> resource. Load the layout resource in your app's
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/app/Activity#onCreate(android.os.Bundle)">Activity.onCreate()</a></code>
callback implementation. Do so by calling
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/app/Activity#setContentView(int)">setContentView()</a></code>,
passing it the reference to your layout resource in the form:
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">R.layout.<em>layout_file_name</em></code>. For example, if your XML
layout is saved as <code translate="no" dir="ltr">main_layout.xml</code>, load it for your
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">Activity</code> as follows:</p>

<div>
<div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">fun</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nf">onCreate</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">savedInstanceState</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">:</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">Bundle</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-k">super</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">onCreate</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">savedInstanceState</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">setContentView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">main_layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-p">}</span></pre></devsite-code>
</section><section><h3 id="java" data-text="Java" tabindex="-1">Java</h3>
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Java"><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">public</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kt">void</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nf">onCreate</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">Bundle</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">savedInstanceState</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">super</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">onCreate</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">savedInstanceState</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">);</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">setContentView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">main_layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">);</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-p">}</span></pre></devsite-code>
</section></div>
</div>

<p>The Android framework calls the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">onCreate()</code> callback method in
your <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Activity</code> when the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Activity</code> launches. For more
information about activity lifecycles, see
<a href="/guide/components/activities#Lifecycle">Introduction to
activities</a>.</p>

<h2 id="attributes" data-text="Attributes" tabindex="-1">Attributes</h2>

<p>Every <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> and <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> object supports its own
variety of XML attributes. Some attributes are specific to a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code>
object. For example, <code translate="no" dir="ltr">TextView</code> supports the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">textSize</code>
attribute. However, these attributes are also inherited by any <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code>
objects that extend this class. Some are common to all <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code>
objects, because they are inherited from the root <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> class, like
the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">id</code> attribute. Other attributes are considered <i>layout
parameters</i>, which are attributes that describe certain layout orientations
of the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> object, as defined by that object's parent
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> object.</p>

<h3 id="id" data-text="ID" tabindex="-1">ID</h3>

<p>Any <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> object can have an integer ID associated with it to
uniquely identify the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> within the tree. When the app is
compiled, this ID is referenced as an integer, but the ID is typically assigned
in the layout XML file as a string in the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">id</code> attribute. This is an
XML attribute common to all <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> objects, and it is defined by the
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> class. You use it very often. The syntax for an ID inside an
XML tag is the following:</p>

<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="XML">android:id="&#64;+id/my_button"</pre></devsite-code>

<p>The  <i>at</i> symbol (&#64;) at the beginning of the string indicates that
the XML parser parses and expands the rest of the ID string and identifies it as
an ID resource. The <i>plus</i> symbol (+) means this is a new resource name
that must be created and added to your resources in the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">R.java</code>
file.</p>

<p>The Android framework offers many other ID resources. When referencing an
Android resource ID, you don't need the <i>plus</i> symbol, but you must add the
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">android</code> package namespace as follows:</p>

<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="XML">android:id="&#64;android:id/empty"</pre></devsite-code>

<p>The <code translate="no" dir="ltr">android</code> package namespace indicates that you're referencing
an ID from the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">android.R</code> resources class, rather than the local
resources class.</p>

<p>To create views and reference them from your app, you can use a common
pattern as follows:</p>

<ol>
  <li>Define a view in the layout file and assign it a unique ID, as in the
  following example:
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="XML">&lt;Button<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>android:id="&#64;+id/my_button"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">        </span>android:layout_width="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">        </span>android:layout_height="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">        </span>android:text="&#64;string/my_button_text"/></pre></devsite-code>
  </li>
  <li>Create an instance of the view object and capture it from the layout,
  typically in the
  <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/app/Activity#onCreate(android.os.Bundle)">onCreate()</a></code>
  method, as shown in the following example:

<div>
<div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">myButton</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">:</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">Button</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">findViewById</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">my_button</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span></pre></devsite-code>
</section><section><h3 id="java" data-text="Java" tabindex="-1">Java</h3>
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Java"><span class="devsite-syntax-n">Button</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">myButton</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">Button</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">findViewById</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">my_button</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">);</span></pre></devsite-code>
</section></div>
</div>
  </li>
</ol>

<p>Defining IDs for view objects is important when creating a
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/RelativeLayout">RelativeLayout</a></code>.
In a relative layout, sibling views can define their layout relative to another
sibling view, which is referenced by the unique ID.</p>

<p>An ID doesn't need to be unique throughout the entire tree, but it must be
unique within the part of the tree you search. It might often be the entire
tree, so it's best to make it unique when possible.</p>

<h3 id="layout-params" data-text="Layout parameters" tabindex="-1">Layout parameters</h3>

<p>XML layout attributes named <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_<em>something</em></code> define
layout parameters for the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code> that are appropriate for the
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> it resides in.</p>

<p>Every <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> class implements a nested class that extends
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/ViewGroup.LayoutParams">ViewGroup.LayoutParams</a></code>.
This subclass contains property types that define the size and position of each
child view, as appropriate for the view group. As shown in figure 2, the parent
view group defines layout parameters for each child view, including the child
view group.</p>

<figure>
  <img src="/static/images/layoutparams.png" alt width="584">
  <figcaption><b>Figure 2.</b> Visualization of a view hierarchy with layout
  parameters associated with each view.</figcaption>
</figure>

<p>Every <code translate="no" dir="ltr">LayoutParams</code> subclass has its own syntax for setting
values. Each child element must define a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">LayoutParams</code> that is
appropriate for its parent, though it might also define a different
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">LayoutParams</code> for its own children.</p>

<p>All view groups include a width and height, using <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_width</code>
and <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_height</code>, and each view is required to define them. Many
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">LayoutParams</code> include optional margins and borders.<p>

<p>You can specify width and height with exact measurements, but you might not
want to do this often. More often, you use one of these constants to set the
width or height:</p>

<ul>
  <li><code translate="no" dir="ltr">wrap_content</code>: tells your view to size itself to the
  dimensions required by its content.</li>
  <li><code translate="no" dir="ltr">match_parent</code>: tells your view to become as big as its parent
  view group allows.</li>
</ul>

<p>In general, we don't recommend specifying a layout width and height using
absolute units such as pixels. A better approach is using relative measurements,
such as density-independent pixel units (dp), <code translate="no" dir="ltr">wrap_content</code>, or
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">match_parent</code>, because it helps your app display properly across a
variety of device screen sizes. The accepted measurement types are defined in
<a href="/guide/topics/resources/layout-resource">Layout resource</a>.</p>


<h2 id="Position" data-text="Layout position" tabindex="-1">Layout position</h2>

<p>A view has rectangular geometry. It has a location, expressed as a pair of
<em>left</em> and <em>top</em> coordinates, and two dimensions, expressed as a
width and height. The unit for location and dimensions is the pixel.</p>

<p>You can retrieve the location of a view by invoking the methods
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getLeft()">getLeft()</a></code>
and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getTop()">getTop()</a></code>.
The former returns the left (<i>x</i>) coordinate of the rectangle representing
the view. The latter returns the top (<i>y</i>) coordinate of the rectangle
representing the view. These methods return the location of the view relative to
its parent. For example, when <code translate="no" dir="ltr">getLeft()</code> returns 20, this means the
view is located 20 pixels to the right of the left edge of its direct
parent.</p>

<p>In addition, there are convenience methods to avoid unnecessary computations:
namely
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getRight()">getRight()</a></code>
and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getBottom()">getBottom()</a></code>.
These methods return the coordinates of the right and bottom edges of the
rectangle representing the view. For example, calling <code translate="no" dir="ltr">getRight()</code> is
similar to the following computation: <code translate="no" dir="ltr">getLeft() + getWidth()</code>.</p>

<h2 id="SizePaddingMargins" data-text="Size, padding, and margins" tabindex="-1">Size, padding, and margins</h2>

<p>The size of a view is expressed with a width and height. A view has two pairs
of width and height values.</p>

<p>The first pair is known as <em>measured width</em> and
<em>measured height</em>. These dimensions define how big a view wants to be
within its parent. You can obtain the measured dimensions by calling
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getMeasuredWidth()">getMeasuredWidth()</a></code>
and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getMeasuredHeight()">getMeasuredHeight()</a></code>.</p>

<p>The second pair is known as <em>width</em> and <em>height</em>, or sometimes
<em>drawing width</em> and <em>drawing height</em>. These dimensions define the
actual size of the view on screen, at drawing time and after layout. These
values might, but don't have to, differ from the measured width and height. You
can obtain the width and height by calling
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getWidth()">getWidth()</a></code>
and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getHeight()">getHeight()</a></code>.</p>

<p>To measure its dimensions, a view takes into account its padding. The padding
is expressed in pixels for the left, top, right and bottom parts of the view.
You can use padding to offset the content of the view by a specific number of
pixels. For instance, a left padding of two pushes the view's content two pixels
to the right of the left edge. You can set padding using the
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#setPadding(int, int, int, int)">setPadding(int, int, int, int)</a></code>
method and query it by calling
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getPaddingLeft()">getPaddingLeft()</a></code>,
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getPaddingTop()">getPaddingTop()</a></code>,
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getPaddingRight()">getPaddingRight()</a></code>,
and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/View#getPaddingBottom()">getPaddingBottom()</a></code>.</p>

<p>Although a view can define a padding, it doesn't support margins. However,
view groups do support margins. See
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/ViewGroup">ViewGroup</a></code> and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/view/ViewGroup.MarginLayoutParams">ViewGroup.MarginLayoutParams</a></code>
for more information.</p>

<p>For more information about dimensions, see
<a href="/guide/topics/resources/more-resources#Dimension">Dimension</a>.</p>

<p>Besides setting margins and padding programmatically, you can also set them
in your XML layouts, as shown in the following example:</p>

  <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="XML"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">  </span>&lt;?xml<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>version="1.0"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>encoding="utf-8"?>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">  </span>&lt;LinearLayout<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>xmlns:android="http://schemas.android.com/apk/res/android"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:layout_width="match_parent"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:layout_height="match_parent"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:orientation="vertical"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">      </span>&lt;TextView<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>android:id="@+id/text"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:layout_width="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:layout_height="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">  <b>              </span>android:layout_margin="16dp"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:padding="8dp"</b>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                </span>android:text="Hello,<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>I<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>am<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>a<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>TextView"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>/>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">      </span>&lt;Button<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>android:id="@+id/button"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:layout_width="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:layout_height="wrap_content"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">  <b>            </span>android:layout_marginTop="16dp"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:paddingBottom="4dp"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:paddingEnd="8dp"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:paddingStart="8dp"
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:paddingTop="4dp"</b>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">              </span>android:text="Hello,<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>I<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>am<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>a<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>Button"<span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span>/>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">  </span>&lt;/LinearLayout>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">  </span></pre></devsite-code>

<p>The preceding example shows margin and padding being applied. The
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">TextView</code> has uniform margins and padding applied all around, and
the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Button</code> shows how you can apply them independently to different
edges.</p>

<aside class="note">
  <b>Note:</b> It is good practice to use <code translate="no" dir="ltr">paddingStart</code>,
  <code translate="no" dir="ltr">paddingEnd</code>, <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_marginStart</code>, and
  <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_marginEnd</code> instead of <code translate="no" dir="ltr">paddingLeft</code>,
  <code translate="no" dir="ltr">paddingRight</code>, <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_marginLeft</code>, and
  <code translate="no" dir="ltr">layout_marginRight</code>, as these behave better with both
  left-to-right and right-to-left language scripts.
</aside>

<style type="text/css">
div.layout {
  float:left;
  width:200px;
  margin:0 0 20px 20px;
}
div.layout.first {
  margin-left:0;
  clear:left;
}
</style>

<h2 id="CommonLayouts" data-text="Common layouts" tabindex="-1">Common layouts</h2>

<p>Each subclass of the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ViewGroup</code> class provides a unique way to
display the views you nest within it. The most flexible layout type, and the one
that provides the best tools for keeping your layout hierarchy shallow, is
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/constraint-layout">ConstraintLayout</a></code>.

<p>The following are some of the common layout types built into the Android
platform.</p>

<aside class="note">
  <b>Note:</b> Although you can nest one or more layouts within another layout
  to achieve your UI design, keep your layout hierarchy as shallow as possible.
  Your layout draws faster if it has fewer nested layouts. A wide view hierarchy
  is better than a deep view hierarchy.
</aside>

<h2 id="framelayout" data-text="FrameLayout" tabindex="-1">FrameLayout</h2>
<p><code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/FrameLayout">FrameLayout</a></code> is the simplest type of layout
object. It's basically a blank space on your screen that you can
later fill with a single object &mdash; for example, a picture that you'll swap in and out.
All child elements of the FrameLayout are pinned to the top left corner of the screen; you cannot
specify a different location for a child view. Subsequent child views will simply be drawn over
previous ones,
partially or totally obscuring them (unless the newer object is transparent).
</p>

<div class="layout first">
  <b><a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/linear">Create a linear layout</a></b>
  <a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/linear"><img src="/static/images/ui/linearlayout-small.png" alt></a>
  <p>Organizes its children into a single horizontal or vertical row and creates
  a scrollbar if the length of the window exceeds the length of the screen.</p>
</div>

<div class="layout">
  <b><a href="/guide/webapps/webview">Build web apps in WebView</a></b>
  <a href="/guide/webapps/webview"><img src="/static/images/ui/webview-small.png"
alt="" /></a>
  <p>Displays web pages.</p>
</div>




<h2 id="AdapterViews" style="clear:left" data-text="Build dynamic lists" tabindex="-1">Build dynamic lists</h2>

<p>When the content for your layout is dynamic or not pre-determined, you can
use
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/recyclerview">RecyclerView</a></code> or
a subclass of
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/binding">AdapterView</a></code>.
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">RecyclerView</code> is generally the better option, because it uses memory
more efficiently than <code translate="no" dir="ltr">AdapterView</code>.</p>

<p>Common layouts possible with <code translate="no" dir="ltr">RecyclerView</code> and
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">AdapterView</code> include the following:</p>

  <div class="layout first"><b><a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/recyclerview">List</a></b>
    <a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/recyclerview"><img src="/static/images/ui/listview-small.png" alt></a>
    <p>Displays a scrolling single column list.</p>
  </div>

  <div class="layout"><b><a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/recyclerview">Grid</a></b>
    <a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/recyclerview"><img src="/static/images/ui/gridview-small.png" alt></a>
    <p>Displays a scrolling grid of columns and rows.</p>
  </div>

<p style="clear:left"><code translate="no" dir="ltr">RecyclerView</code> offers more possibilities and
the option to
<a href="/develop/ui/views/layout/recyclerview#plan-your-layout">create a custom
layout manager</a>.</p>

<h3 id="FillingTheLayout" style="clear:left" data-text="Fill an adapter view with data" tabindex="-1">Fill an adapter view with data</h3>

<p>You can populate an
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/AdapterView">AdapterView</a></code>
such as <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/ListView">ListView</a></code>
or
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/GridView">GridView</a></code> by
binding the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">AdapterView</code> instance to an
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/Adapter">Adapter</a></code>,
which retrieves data from an external source and creates a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">View</code>
that represents each data entry.</p>

<p>Android provides several subclasses of <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Adapter</code> that are useful
for retrieving different kinds of data and building views for an
<code translate="no" dir="ltr">AdapterView</code>. The two most common adapters are:</p>

<dl>
  <dt><code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/ArrayAdapter">ArrayAdapter</a></code></dt>
    <dd>Use this adapter when your data source is an array. By default,
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ArrayAdapter</code> creates a view for each array item by calling
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/java/lang/Object#toString()">toString()</a></code>
    on each item and placing the contents in a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">TextView</code>.
    <p>For example, if you have an array of strings you want to display in a
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ListView</code>, initialize a new <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ArrayAdapter</code> using a
    constructor to specify the layout for each string and the string array:</p>

    <div>
    <div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
    <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">adapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ArrayAdapter&lt;String&gt;</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-k">this</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">android</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">simple_list_item_1</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">myStringArray</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>

<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span></pre></devsite-code>
    </section></div>
    </div>
    <p>The arguments for this constructor are the following:</p>

    <ul>
      <li>Your app
      <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/content/Context">Context</a></code></li>
      <li>The layout that contains a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">TextView</code> for each string in the
      array</li>
      <li>The string array</li>
    </ul>

    <p>Then call
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/AdapterView#setAdapter(T)">setAdapter()</a></code>
    on your <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ListView</code>:</p>

    <div>
    <div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
    <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">:</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ListView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">findViewById</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">listview</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>

<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span></pre></devsite-code>
    </section></div>
    </div>

    <p>To customize the appearance of each item you can override the
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">toString()</code> method for the objects in your array. Or, to create
    a view for each item that's something other than a
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">TextView</code>&mdash;for example, if you want an
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/ImageView">ImageView</a></code>
    for each array item&mdash;extend the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">ArrayAdapter</code> class and
    override
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/ArrayAdapter#getView(int, android.view.View, android.view.ViewGroup)">getView()</a></code>
    to return the type of view you want for each item.</p>
    </dd>

  <dt><code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/SimpleCursorAdapter">SimpleCursorAdapter</a></code></dt>
    <dd>Use this adapter when your data comes from a
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/database/Cursor">Cursor</a></code>.
    When using <code translate="no" dir="ltr">SimpleCursorAdapter</code>, specify a layout to use for
    each row in the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Cursor</code> and which columns in the
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Cursor</code> you want inserted into the views of the layout you want.
    For example, if you want to create a list of people's names and phone
    numbers, you can perform a query that returns a <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Cursor</code>
    containing a row for each person and columns for the names and numbers. You
    then create a string array specifying which columns from the
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Cursor</code> you want in the layout for each result and an integer
    array specifying the corresponding views that each column need to be
    placed:</p>

    <div>
    <div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
    <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">fromColumns</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">arrayOf</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ContactsContract</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">Data</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">DISPLAY_NAME</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                              </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ContactsContract</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">CommonDataKinds</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">Phone</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">NUMBER</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">toViews</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">intArrayOf</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">display_name</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">phone_number</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span></pre></devsite-code>
    </section><section><h3 id="java" data-text="Java" tabindex="-1">Java</h3>
    <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Java"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">String</span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">[]</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">fromColumns</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ContactsContract</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">Data</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">DISPLAY_NAME</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">                            </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ContactsContract</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">CommonDataKinds</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">Phone</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">NUMBER</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">};</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kt">int</span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">[]</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">toViews</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">display_name</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">phone_number</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">};</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span></pre></devsite-code>
    </section></div>
    </div>
    <p>When you instantiate the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">SimpleCursorAdapter</code>, pass the
    layout to use for each result, the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Cursor</code> containing the
    results, and these two arrays:</p>

    <div>
    <div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
    <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">adapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">SimpleCursorAdapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-k">this</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">            </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">person_name_and_number</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">cursor</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">fromColumns</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">toViews</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-m">0</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">val</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nv">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">getListView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">()</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">adapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">adapter</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span></pre></devsite-code>
    </section><section><h3 id="java" data-text="Java" tabindex="-1">Java</h3>
    <div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Java"><span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">SimpleCursorAdapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">adapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-k">new</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">SimpleCursorAdapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-k">this</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">            </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">R</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">layout</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">person_name_and_number</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">cursor</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">fromColumns</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">toViews</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-mi">0</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">);</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">ListView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">getListView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">();</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">setAdapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">adapter</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">);</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span></pre></devsite-code>
    </section></div>
    </div>

    <p>The <code translate="no" dir="ltr">SimpleCursorAdapter</code> then creates a view for each row in
    the <code translate="no" dir="ltr">Cursor</code> using the provided layout by inserting each
    <code translate="no" dir="ltr">fromColumns</code> item into the corresponding <code translate="no" dir="ltr">toViews</code>
    view.</p></dd>
</dl>

<p>If during the course of your app's life you change the underlying data that
is read by your adapter, call
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/ArrayAdapter#notifyDataSetChanged()">notifyDataSetChanged()</a></code>.
This notifies the attached view that the data has been changed and it refreshes
itself.</p>

<h3 id="HandlingUserSelections" data-text="Handle click events" tabindex="-1">Handle click events</h3>

<p>You can respond to click events on each item in an <code translate="no" dir="ltr">AdapterView</code>
by implementing the
<code translate="no" dir="ltr"><a href="/reference/android/widget/AdapterView.OnItemClickListener">AdapterView.OnItemClickListener</a></code>
interface. For example:</p>

<div>
<div class="ds-selector-tabs"><section><h3 id="kotlin" data-text="Kotlin" tabindex="-1">Kotlin</h3>
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Kotlin"><span class="devsite-syntax-n">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">onItemClickListener</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">AdapterView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">OnItemClickListener</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">parent</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">view</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">position</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">-</span>&gt;
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-c1">// Do something in response to the click.</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-p">}</span></pre></devsite-code>
</section><section><h3 id="java" data-text="Java" tabindex="-1">Java</h3>
<div></div><devsite-code><pre class="devsite-click-to-copy" translate="no" dir="ltr" is-upgraded syntax="Java"><span class="devsite-syntax-c1">// Create a message handling object as an anonymous class.</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-kd">private</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">OnItemClickListener</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">messageClickedHandler</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-o">=</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-k">new</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">OnItemClickListener</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">()</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kd">public</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kt">void</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-nf">onItemClick</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">AdapterView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">parent</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">View</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">v</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kt">int</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">position</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">,</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-kt">long</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">id</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">)</span><span class="devsite-syntax-w"> </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">{</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">        </span><span class="devsite-syntax-c1">// Do something in response to the click.</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-w">    </span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">}</span>
<span class="devsite-syntax-p">};</span>

<span class="devsite-syntax-n">listView</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">.</span><span class="devsite-syntax-na">setOnItemClickListener</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">(</span><span class="devsite-syntax-n">messageClickedHandler</span><span class="devsite-syntax-p">);</span></pre></devsite-code>
</section></div>
</div>
